# Agent 1 — Bank Disclosures — Findings 2026-07-30

(Method: shared research harness — one agent per bank, adversarial per-claim verification, completeness gate, DETERMINISTIC table render — run in batches and merged deterministically in Python. 42 banks targeted, 34 returned verified signals, 455 verified signal rows. Confidence capped at Medium per single-agent playbook; only the Orchestrator promotes to High after cross-agent confirmation. Rows ordered by Opp.-size band, biddable first.)

> **⚠ COVERAGE WARNING:** 8 of 42 banks returned NO verified signals in this run — treat as genuine gaps, NOT "nothing happening". Most were cut off by an API session limit rather than by an exhausted search; see *Run integrity* below: **Addiko Group (HR/SI/RS/BA/ME), Erste Group (multi-country deck), Groups: OTP / Intesa Sanpaolo CEE / NLB / UniCredit, KBC Group (CZ/SK/HU/BG), Montenegro (CKB, NLB, Hipotekarna, Lovcen, Prva, Adriatic, Erste), North Macedonia (Komercijalna, Stopanska/NBG, NLB, Halkbank, Sparkasse, UNIBank, ProCredit, Silk Road), ProCredit Holding (XK/BA/BG/RO/RS/AL/UA/MK), RBI Group (multi-country)**.

## ⚠ Run integrity — read before using this file

This run was degraded by repeated API session limits, not by exhausted research. It is reported here because a coverage gap that looks like "nothing happening in that market" is the exact failure this harness exists to prevent.

- The first attempt (all 42 banks in one call) died with 127 of 132 agents erroring. It returned a valid document with an **empty table and all 42 banks flagged EMPTY**. That output was NOT written to this file — it would have replaced real findings with a false footprint-wide blackout.
- The roster was then split into batches. Batch 1 (18 units) verified 6 before the limit hit; batch 2 (12 units) verified 0. Four separate limit hits occurred across the run.
- Diagnosis: the harness checkpoints **research** to `parts/<runDate>/<slug>.json` but holds **verification** only in memory. Every limit hit therefore destroyed the verify stage while leaving expensive, already-paid-for research stranded on disk and invisible — 446 researched signals across 34 units, against 164 that had reached a document.
- Recovery: a new `weekly-intelligence/workflows/verify-checkpoints.workflow.js` runs verification ONLY, reading the stranded checkpoints and skipping re-research. Each agent writes its verified JSON to `parts/<runDate>/verified/<slug>.json` **before returning**, so progress survives a kill. It recovered **22 units / 291 signals** across two passes — two of which (Slovenia, Raiffeisen Serbia) existed only because of that write-before-return step.
- Final state: **34 of 42 banks covered, 455 verified signals.** The table was assembled by concatenating deterministically-rendered rows in Python; no LLM ever wrote or re-wrote a table row.

**Consequence for the reader:** every row below has been adversarially verified. But the 8 banks marked ⚠ EMPTY were **cut off mid-run, not researched and found quiet.** Do not read their absence as an absence of activity.

**Still outstanding (carry to the next run):** Montenegro and North Macedonia were never researched. All six regional-group units — Erste, KBC, RBI, OTP/Intesa/NLB/UniCredit, plus the newly-added Addiko and ProCredit — were never reached, so **none of the country-level fleet figures in this file have been reconciled against group-level disclosure.** Treat per-country counts as un-cross-checked.

**Recommended fix to the harness:** checkpoint verified output per unit exactly as research is checkpointed. Had that existed, the first failure would have cost minutes rather than three re-runs.

## What changed since last run

The most valuable new material this run is **structural cash-automation white space, quantified from banks' own live data rather than from press copy.** Three locator-level pulls now size the recycler gap precisely: **UBB Bulgaria** publishes 738 ATM sites of which only 246 accept cash deposits — roughly 490 cash-out-only sites — against its own disclosure that only BGN 406m came in through deposit ATMs while BGN 2.3bn was handled at branch counters in the first five months of euro operation. **Postbank Bulgaria** shows 498 ATM locations of which 225 are deposit-capable. **DSK's** feed returns 817 locations, 726 carrying an ATM. In each case the deposit-capable share is well under half, and in each case the bank has published transaction evidence that counter volume is the expensive alternative. That is the clearest quantified recycler case the agent has assembled for Bulgaria.

The same pattern appears in the Adriatic and CEE clusters. **Erste Croatia's** own locator API returned 666 ATMs on 31/07/2026 of which only 199 are deposit-capable. In **Czechia**, the pooled four-bank shared network is over 1,900 ATMs with roughly 960 accepting deposits and nearly 800 recycling, while the sector census dated 01/01/2026 shows 1,426 branches of which only 548 still have a manned cash desk — cash is being pushed out of branches faster than deposit automation is being installed. **Croatia** is the one to watch structurally: HNB reports 4,054 ATMs at 31/03/2026, and banks are negotiating a shared national ATM network as the cost-sharing mechanism for a statutory access right effective **01/01/2027** — a concrete, dated trigger inside the 18-month window.

In the Western Balkans the regulatory clock moved. The **TIPS Clone** instant-payment platform went live on **20/07/2026** for Bosnia and Kosovo. Bosnia's IPS scheme rulebook was set by CBBiH board decision of **10/07/2026**, the FBiH House of Representatives adopted the harmonised financial laws on **28/07/2026**, and installed ATMs rose to **1,868 at 31/12/2025** from 1,767. Separately, FATF placed Bosnia on its increased-monitoring "grey list" on **19/06/2026**, which typically pulls AML/monitoring spend forward. **Albania** now carries a universal POS/POI acceptance obligation enforced by the tax administration.

On the Romanian side, **CEC Bank** confirmed its estate at 1,220 ATMs plus 264 multifunction machines at 31/12/2025 and launched five pilot Digital Agencies on 05/06/2026, with its Remote Operations director stating on 06/04/2026 that the bank does **not** intend to close branches — so the Romanian opportunity is automation inside a retained network, not consolidation.

**Stale figures killed this run.** A targeted pass over four long-suspect items produced more value than most new research. **BRD's "~1,400 ATMs / ~1,600 terminals" is dead** — the verifier traced it to boilerplate in a press release dated 15/09/2021 and refused to carry it; BRD's own June-2026 board report instead confirms **334 branches at 30/06/2026** (from 347 at 31/12/2025 and 357 at 30/06/2025) and H1-2026 investment of **RON 118m Group / RON 112m Bank**, up from ~RON 100m a year earlier. **BCR's 2016-vintage 2,616-machine breakdown was not carried**, but NCR Atleos's own page confirms verbatim that BCR runs "more than 2,600 ATMs and its cash processing centers with the help of NCR Corporation and its channel partner Printec" — the incumbency is now primary-sourced rather than inferred. For **AikBank**, the previously-unreadable locator turned out to be machine-readable at `aikbank.rs/data/maps-formated.json`, which closes a standing operator request, and NBS data gives Serbia **3,336 ATMs / 187,957 POS at Q1-2026**. **Eurobank Cyprus's "Cash 360" figures remain unusable** — the 11-coin/5-note counts are still Oct-2022 Hellenic Bank vintage, the vendor is still undisclosed, and one carried item was re-dated to 12/09/2025 and re-tagged [STANDING].

**Caveat on completeness:** the group-level decks (Erste, KBC, RBI, OTP/Intesa/NLB/UniCredit) and the two newly-added group units (Addiko, ProCredit) were never reached, so **no country-level reconciliation against group disclosure was possible this run** — the per-country fleet numbers above are un-cross-checked. Montenegro and North Macedonia were not reached at all.

## Top opportunities (ranked by evidence and disclosed scale)

*Lane typing (net_new_contest / owned_asset / incumbent_renewal / lost / threat_macro) was not produced by this degraded run — the Orchestrator must assign it before these feed the dashboard's opportunity ranking.*

1. **UBB Bulgaria** — ~490 cash-out-only ATM sites against BGN 406m of ATM deposits versus BGN 2.3bn over the counter; the best-quantified recycler business case in the file (XL).
2. **Czech shared deposit network** — over 1,900 pooled ATMs with only ~960 deposit-capable, against 1,426 branches of which just 548 retain a manned cash desk (XL).
3. **Croatia shared national ATM network** — statutory access right effective 01/01/2027 with cost-sharing under negotiation now; a dated, footprint-relevant trigger (XL).
4. **Erste Croatia** — 666 ATMs, only 199 deposit-capable, on a fleet Printec already services elsewhere in the OTP/Erste Adriatic estate (XL).
5. **Postbank + DSK Bulgaria** — deposit-capable share under half on both estates, both post-euro-changeover, both with the fleet OEM still unidentified (XL).
6. **Bosnia** — instant payments live 20/07/2026, ATM base growing (+101 in 2025), and FATF grey-listing from 19/06/2026 pulling AML and monitoring spend forward (XL).
7. **CEC Bank Romania** — 1,484 machines, five Digital Agencies live, and an explicit statement that branches will not close: automation inside a retained network (L).

Slide 20 repeats: "First Greek bank certified under ISO/IEC 42001, validating enterprise-wide AI governance and controls, strengthening EU AI Act readiness". | [Piraeus Financial Holdings — H1 2026 Press Release (p.15) and Results Presentation (slide 20)](https://www.piraeusgroup.gr/-/jssmedia/Com/2026/Files/Investors/Financials/Financial-Results/H1/Q2_2026-Press-Release.pdf?sc_lang=en) | 29/07/2026 | Piraeus is setting a governance bar its suppliers will be measured against. In plain terms, ISO/IEC 42001 is a management-system standard for how an organisation governs AI, and the EU AI Act is the EU law imposing obligations on higher-risk AI uses. Any Printec component embedding AI/ML — transaction monitoring, AML scoring, eKYC liveness and document checks, ATM predictive maintenance — will need auditable model governance and EU AI Act documentation to clear procurement. This is a qualification gate more than a revenue line. | High that AI-governance evidence becomes a standard procurement requirement within 12 months | Medium | M | Low | Prepare an EU AI Act / ISO 42001-aligned model-governance pack for Printec's AML, transaction-monitoring and eKYC modules before the next Piraeus vendor review. |

| Albanian Tax Administration (DPT) / 2025 tax-procedures amendments (POS/POI mandate) | Albania | Albania has a universal legal obligation for taxpayers to be equipped with POS/POI card-acceptance terminals at every point of sale. The Albanian Tax Administration's own standing notice 'Njoftim per tatimpaguesit qe duhet te pajisen me POS/POI' states verbatim that by 30 MAY 2026 all taxpayers making cash sales in the listed activities must be equipped with POS/POI at every point of sale (accommodation structures, transport, public institutions with state capital); the deadline for ALL REMAINING BUSINESSES is 31 DECEMBER 2026. The same 2025 fiscal package cut the business-to-business cash ceiling from ALL 150,000 to ALL 100,000 (reported as applying from January 2026) and introduced a new ALL 500,000 ceiling on individual-to-business cash payments. Exemptions, verbatim from Monitor: entities operating in areas without internet coverage, those exempt from issuing invoices, and non-VAT self-employed individuals with no more than one place of activity. H1-2025 baseline: 46.4% of business-to-business invoices and 9.7% of value still cash; business-to-individual 90.6% of invoices and 64.2% of value still cash. CORRECTION: the researcher's law number 'Law 79/2025' could NOT be verified - neither the Monitor article nor the Tax Administration's own tax-procedures legislation index cites a Law 79/2025 (that index lists Law 86/2025 as the recent 2025 instrument). Parliament approved tax-procedures amendments in November 2025, but the exact law number remains unestablished; cite the DPT notice and the deadlines, not the law number. [partially-confirmed] | [Albanian Tax Administration (DPT) - 'Njoftim per tatimpaguesit qe duhet te pajisen me POS/POI' (notice 1942); Revista Monitor - 'Ulet kufiri per parane cash, deri ne fund te vitit 2026, te gjithe bizneset me terminale POS'](https://www.tatime.gov.al/d/8/45/0/1942/njoftim-per-tatimpaguesit-qe-duhet-te-pajisen-me-pos-poi) | 05/11/2025 | The single largest terminal-demand event in Albania. CBBiH submitted a DRAFT application on 12/12/2025 and says conditions now exist to file the official application with the European Commission and the European Payments Council. | CBBiH IPS BiH scheme rulebook - 'Odluka o pravilima rada sistema instant placanja' | Bosnia & Herzegovina | The CBBiH Governing Board decision of 10/07/2026 (7th session) sets binding IPS BiH rules. Art. 13: payment orders must be denominated in BAM; the maximum single transaction is 5,000.00 BAM; the MANDATORY channel for initiating a payment is a mobile or electronic payment platform; a QR code may be used to initiate a payment per a specification CBBiH will publish. Art. 12: maximum end-to-end settlement time is 10 seconds, gross, irrevocable and final. Art. 7 makes participation compulsory: any bank that executed at least 1 million transactions cumulatively across the RTGS (BPRV) and giro-clearing systems between 01/01/2026 and 30/06/2026 must be an IPS BiH participant from 01/01/2027; any bank exceeding 500 thousand cumulative transactions in a calendar year must join from 1 July of the following year. Art. 5: a direct participant must have an established ESMIG connection, a payment-operations licence, a reserve account at CBBiH, an IPS BiH settlement account, a signed statement accepting the rulebook, and must pass CBBiH test scenarios. Indirect participants (non-bank payment service providers) additionally need a contract with a direct-participant bank that provides their liquidity. | [CBBiH, 'Odluka o pravilima rada sistema instant placanja Centralne banke Bosne i Hercegovine' (58-page PDF, scanned)](https://www.cbbh.ba/data/dokumenti/pdf/1024-6_Odluka_o_pravilima_rada_instant_pla%C4%87anja.pdf) | 10/07/2026 | A dated compliance cliff: the largest BiH banks are legally obliged to be live on IPS BiH by 01/01/2027, and every joiner needs a mobile or e-payment front end (the rulebook makes this the only permitted initiation channel, so any bank without a working mobile app cannot comply), an ESMIG link, QR acceptance, and 10-second processing with fraud controls that must run inside that 10-second window. Directly addressable by Printec digital onboarding/eKYC, transaction monitoring, AML and HSM/security, and by POS/acquiring for QR merchant acceptance. The Art. 7(2) rolling 500k trigger means the mandate keeps catching smaller banks year after year. | Certain - binding regulatory decision already in force | Medium | XL | Medium | Get CBBiH RTGS/giro statistics for H1-2026 to identify which banks cross the 1m-transaction test and are therefore legally obliged to be live by 01/01/2027; screen those for absence of a mobile payment platform today; watch for publication of the CBBiH QR-code specification. |
| Bosnia & Herzegovina (FATF grey list) / Banking Agency of FBiH (FBA) | Bosnia & Herzegovina | At its plenary of 19/06/2026 the FATF placed Bosnia and Herzegovina on the list of jurisdictions under increased monitoring (the 'grey list' - countries with strategic anti-money-laundering deficiencies that have committed to a time-bound action plan), pointing to needed improvements in preventing money laundering and terrorist financing. The FBA states the banking system was assessed within the MoneyVal evaluation as one of the best-regulated areas in BiH, that the classification is not connected to the stability or quality of the banking system, regulation or supervision under FBA's remit, and that the reasons relate to wider legal and institutional framework questions beyond banking. The FBA nonetheless concedes that, taking account of the MoneyVal findings and the Action Plan measures, the current classification 'may have limited effects on the efficiency of the performance of banking services in international business'. An Action Plan of measures applies. | [Agencija za bankarstvo Federacije BiH (FBA), 'Moneyval: Informacija o procjeni i ogranicenim efektima na bankarski sistem u vezi sa klasifikacijom na sivu listu'](https://www.fba.ba/bs/moneyval-informacija-o-procjeni-i-ogranicenim-efektima-na-bankarski-sistem-u-vezi-sa-klasifikacijom-na-sivu-listu) | 19/06/2026 | Grey-listing pushes every BiH bank and its correspondent banks into enhanced due diligence, stronger sanctions and PEP screening, better suspicious-transaction-report quality and demonstrable transaction monitoring. | Financna sprava SR (eKasa / Act 384/2025 Z.z.) | Slovakia | Act 384/2025 Z.z. on the recording of sales took effect 01/01/2026, replacing the previous eKasa regime. Section 15 obliges every seller subject to sales recording to let the buyer pay cashlessly for any sale exceeding EUR 1 (exempt only where there is no internet signal or a failure of the confirmation technology). The as-published act set this from 01/03/2026; it was postponed to 01/05/2026 by indirect amendment Act 30/2026 Z.z. From 01/01/2026 the old positive annex listing which services must use eKasa is gone - the new act instead defines a short exemption list in Sec. 3(2) (severely disabled sole traders, high-mountain facilities without road/power connection, vending machines, supervised financial-market entities, and utility supply), so in practice all other service providers are captured. A third register type was introduced alongside the online and virtual registers: the 'softvérová on-line registračná pokladnica' (software online cash register), whose till program and protected data storage may be hosted in a remote/cloud environment. Sec. 3(4) requires a notice about the act's obligations at every point of sale, easily accessible and legible, with the template and placement rules set by the Financial Directorate (the widely reported A5 minimum size comes from that guidance, not from the act). PENALTIES, per Sec. 17: for the most serious eKasa breaches (Sec. 16 a-d, g, u - not using an eKasa register, disabling it, using a non-compliant register) EUR 1,500-20,000 on first detection and EUR 3,000-40,000 on each further detection; lesser breaches carry EUR 150-6,500 rising to EUR 300-13,000. For failing to enable cashless payment (Sec. 16 v): NO fine on first detection - the tax or customs office merely instructs the seller of the obligation - then EUR 500-15,000 on second detection and EUR 3,000-40,000 on each further detection. Serious breaches also trigger a sales ban at that point of sale of up to 72 hours and a referral to withdraw the trade licence. [partially-confirmed] | [Zbierka zakonov SR - Act 384/2025 Z.z. (official PDF, Slov-Lex)](https://static.slov-lex.sk/pdf/SK/ZZ/2025/384/ZZ_2025_384_20260101.pdf) | 19/12/2025 | Every Slovak service business now needs an eKasa register AND a card-acceptance route, in force since 01/05/2026 - direct demand for Printec eKasa ECR, Android POS and acquiring enablement. The new cloud-hosted software register type is the format most likely to win the newly captured micro-merchants and is a software/SaaS play rather than a hardware one. The graduated penalty design (warning first, then fines) means the commercial urgency peaks at the second inspection, not the first. | High - in force | Medium | L | High | Read the consolidated post-30/2026 text of Sec. 15 on Slov-Lex to confirm the 01/05/2026 date verbatim in law; obtain the Financial Directorate's published notice template and placement instruction (source of the A5 requirement) |

| Agencija za bankarstvo FBiH (FBA) - 2026 supervisory expectations | Bosnia & Herzegovina | The FBA published 'Supervizorska ocekivanja u vezi sa postupanjem banaka u 2026. godini' (document dated February 2026; FBA notice of publication 12/02/2026) setting 11 supervisory priorities. Item 5 ('Preparing for extreme technology and cyber risk scenarios') requires banks to strengthen digital resilience with regular resilience testing including penetration testing and red-team exercises; to improve ICT security controls through multi-layer protection, data encryption, network segmentation and continuous activity monitoring; to ensure early attack-detection systems, automated responses and business continuity and recovery plans, with continuity plans covering scenarios requiring systems to be rebuilt 'from zero'; and to integrate third-party and technology-disruptor risk into risk assessment, with a clear register of every activity outsourced to external partners. Item 9 (AML/CFT) requires transaction-monitoring systems tuned to real BiH client behaviour - explicitly naming cash flows, remittances from abroad, cross-border payments and financing - with improved analytics and automation but clearly defined human assessment and escalation, so that controls are neither excessively formalistic nor blind to real threats; it also requires improved timeliness and quality of suspicious-transaction reports per the National Risk Assessment, and interlinked sanctions/beneficial-owner/end-use controls for proliferation financing. Item 10 requires banks to define payment-services development goals and activities in their business strategies and to prepare for BiH's accession to SEPA. | Croatia - Ministry of Finance / HNB (free basic services package) | Croatia | Croatia's free-basic-services-package regime (Ministry of Finance Q&A dated 4 veljace 2026 = 04/02/2026) confirms, verbatim: the package must include services enabling cash DEPOSIT to the payment account at a counter OR at an ATM, explicitly excluding coin processing ('osim obrade kovanog novca'); free cash withdrawal at the counter or ATM of the account-holding bank; free internet OR mobile banking at the bank's choice; and free debit-card issuance and use at physical points of sale. From 01/01/2027 a package holder additionally gets two free cash withdrawals per month at OTHER banks' ATMs in Croatia (same right for vulnerable-group basic-account holders). The bank's deadline to act on a customer request falls from 30 days to 10 working days on 02/07/2026. The package is NOT automatic - it applies only on customer request, for one euro account receiving regular income. The commercial driver is that free-at-the-counter is the expensive way to comply, so banks are pushed to steer free deposits onto cheaper self-service: deposit ATMs, recyclers, cash-cycle optimisation and branch transformation. The coin carve-out makes coin-deposit machines a chargeable add-on and therefore a saleable product rather than a compliance cost. The 01/01/2027 cross-bank withdrawal right is the bigger structural driver (see the national shared ATM network signal). | Government of Kosovo / Assembly - Draft Law on Payment Services | Kosovo | A new Draft Law on Payment Services was approved at the 8th meeting of the Government of the Republic of Kosovo by Decision No. 08/08 dated 03/04/2026 (255-page trilingual Albanian/English/Serbian text). Its Article 3 states it transposes EU Directive 2015/2366 (PSD2), Directive 2009/110/EC (e-money), Directive 2014/92/EU (payment accounts) and Regulation (EU) 2024/886 on INSTANT CREDIT TRANSFERS IN EURO. Concrete provisions relevant to self-service: (a) Art. 1.29 defines 'advanced customer authentication' as authentication using two or more independent elements from knowledge / possession / inherence, with elements that dynamically link the transaction to a specific amount and payee; (b) Art. 1.15 EXCLUDES from the law's scope 'cash withdrawal services offered by means of ATM by providers, acting on behalf of one or more card issuers, which are not a party to the framework contract with the customer', on condition that they provide no other payment services and that the customer is still given information on withdrawal charges - i.e. an independent (non-bank) ATM deployer can operate in Kosovo without a payment-institution authorisation; (c) Art. 59.2 requires that where currency conversion (DCC) is offered before a transaction is initiated at an ATM, at a point of sale or by the payee, the party offering it must disclose to the payer ALL charges and the exchange rate to be used. CONTEXT ADDED BY VERIFIER: this is a re-draft. On 09/01/2026 Kosovo's Constitutional Court struck down the Assembly's December-2024 approval of the previous Law on Payment Services (one of 12 laws voided on a PDK complaint), so this law has to go through the Assembly again. | [Office of the Prime Minister of Kosovo - PROJEKTLIGJI PER SHERBIMET E PAGESAVE / Draft Law on Payment Services](https://kryeministri.rks-gov.net/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/PROJEKTLIGJI-PER-SHERBIMET-E-PAGESAVE.pdf) | 03/04/2026 | Compliance-driven refresh of the whole self-service and acquiring estate: strong/advanced customer authentication on cards and channels, DCC screen-flow changes on every ATM and POS in the country, and a legal route for independent non-bank ATM operators - a new customer class for Printec ATM-as-a-service and managed services. Because the previous version was voided, this is also the single legal gate on Kosovo's SEPA accession, which raises its political priority. | High for eventual enactment (SEPA membership depends on it); Medium on timing - it has to clear the Assembly again after the Constitutional Court annulment | Medium | L | Medium | Track Assembly adoption and then the CBK secondary regulations, which will carry the actual compliance dates for the SCA and ATM/DCC articles. | Assembly of Kosovo / CBK - Law on Banks and SEPA accession | Kosovo | MATERIALLY CORRECTED. Law No. 08/L-304 on Banks (adopted by the Assembly 05/12/2024, promulgated by Presidential Decree No. DL-16/2026 dated 20/01/2026) was published in Official Gazette of the Republic of Kosovo No. 2 of 27/01/2026 and, per its Article 150, entered into force 15 days after publication (i.e. ~11/02/2026). Article 1.2 states it is harmonised with Regulation 575/2013/EU (CRR), Directive 2013/36/EU (CRD) and Directive 2014/59/EU (BRRD). On SEPA: CBK told Radio Free Europe that the pre-application was completed on 14/10/2024 and updated on 20/12/2024, that the European Commission accepted the documentation, and that 'the final application can only be submitted after the laws enter into force'. CBK estimates SEPA membership would save Kosovo citizens and businesses about EUR 55 million a year. THE CORRECTION: the researcher framed the Law on Banks as clearing the SEPA blocker. It only clears ONE of three. On 09/01/2026 the Constitutional Court ruled that the Assembly's approval of the Law on Banks WAS constitutional, but struck down 12 other laws from the same December-2024 batch - including the Law on Payment Services. So the SEPA gate is now the Payment Services law (and the AML law), not the Law on Banks, and the final SEPA application had still not been filed as of the last verifiable statement. The EUR 55m/yr CBK savings estimate is the political forcing function. | CEC Bank / all ATM operators in Romania | Romania | Legislative proposal B409/2026, amending Law 209/2019 on payment services, was registered at the Senate (first chamber) on 24/06/2026 with 84 deputy signatories spanning PSD (36), AUR (23), unaffiliated (7), UDMR (6), USR (3), PNL (2), POT (2) and minority/other groups. A new Art. 221^1 would oblige every payment service provider operating cash-withdrawal ATMs in Romania to let users key in a personalised withdrawal amount, for all cardholders of PSPs authorised in Romania regardless of card issuer, and expressly states that offering fast-withdrawal preset-value buttons cannot substitute for that obligation, subject to the available cash and the banknote mix in the machine. | Hungary retail cash policy | Hungary | From February 2026 the statutory free monthly ATM cash-withdrawal allowance on Hungarian household forint accounts doubled from HUF 150,000 to HUF 300,000, still capped at two transactions per month and still requiring the customer to file a declaration with one nominated bank; the allowance is usable at any domestic bank's ATM. The law change was published in Magyar Kozlony in December 2025. Banks, not customers, bear the 0.9% cash-withdrawal transaction levy, which is uncapped - so doubling the free ceiling directly increases bank cost per withdrawal. | MNB decree 19/2025 (VI.26.) - Phase 2 and reallocation mechanics | Hungary | Consolidated text of MNB decree 19/2025 (VI.26.), in force from 27/06/2025. Allocation weights: 2024 payment-service fee revenue 20%; debit cards with a cash-withdrawal function issued as at 31/12/2024 30%; consumer payment accounts at 31/12/2024 10%; retail sight forint deposits with a non-zero balance at 31/12/2024 15%; balance-sheet total at 31/12/2024 25% - assessed at group level for banking groups, then overlaid with per-settlement macro criteria (district gross average earnings, settlement population, unemployment rate, share of residents employed off-settlement). Annex 1 names the obligated bank for every affected settlement and IS PUBLIC. Section 3(3)(b): banks wishing to SWAP settlement packages with each other or take over one another's installation/operation duty for the Phase 2 (over-500-population) wave had to sign those INTER-BANK contracts by 28/02/2026 and report them into the MNB's ERA system within 5 working days, and by 16/03/2026 at the latest. Section 3(2): a contract taking over ATM OPERATION cannot be concluded for less than 5 years. Section 4(4): a newly designated operator must reimburse the previous installer's demonstrably incurred installation costs (those incurred within 3 years). Sections 4(3) and 6: the MNB re-runs the whole allocation every 3 years, first review due around 27/06/2028. [contradicted] | [Wolters Kluwer Jogtar - 19/2025. (VI. 26.) MNB rendelet (consolidated)](https://net.jogtar.hu/jogszabaly?docid=a2500019.mnb) | 27/06/2025 | Two durable commercial hooks. First, the 5-year minimum term on operation-takeover contracts plus the 3-yearly statutory reallocation means ATM operation in rural Hungary is a recurring, contractually long site-transfer business rather than a one-off install - and the next reallocation lands around mid-2028. Second, the cost-reimbursement rule at s.4(4) means an incoming bank inherits a priced asset, which favours standardised, well-documented estates and a single service partner across banks. Annex 1 being public means Printec can build the full settlement-by-settlement target map today. | High - in force, with the next statutory reallocation due around 27/06/2028 | Medium | M | Medium | Extract the full Annex 1 settlement-to-bank map from jogtar and build the territory plan; note the ERA-reported contract register would only reveal inter-bank reallocations, NOT vendor selections, so it is a lower-value ask than the original pass assumed. |

| National Bank of Ukraine (open banking) - affects Oschadbank, Raiffeisen Ukraine and every Ukrainian bank | Ukraine | Ukraine's open-banking regime hits its operative deadline NOW, not in 2025. The framework rests on NBU Board Resolutions No. 80 (Положення про відкритий банкінг в Україні), No. 81 (authorisation of non-financial payment service providers) and No. 82 (electronic trust services / qualified open-banking certificates), all dated 25/07/2025, sitting on top of Resolution No. 58 of 03/05/2023 (authentication and strong customer authentication on the payment market) and Resolution No. 43 of 19/05/2021 (information protection and cyber-defence for payment-market participants). THE TRIGGER: NBU Deputy Governor Oleksii Shaban stated on 26/06/2026 that practically the entire Ukrainian banking system will open its APIs in August-September 2026, with full open-banking launch in autumn 2026 and certain technical API requirements taking effect from 01/08/2026. As at 31/07/2026 the NBU register shows 6 authorisation entries - 3 PISP (payment-initiation) and 3 AISP (account-information); as at 26/06/2026 the authorised institutions were PrivatBank, PUMB and UkrSibBank (PUMB was first, in March 2026), plus two approved technology operators, Ukrainian Processing Center and TAS LINK. Every bank is an ASPSP and must, per the NBU's own published checklist: (a) run base APIs giving TPPs continuous real-time access to customer accounts, (b) publish full API technical documentation - protocols, procedures, tooling - on its own website, and (c) verify each TPP's authorisation against the Payment Infrastructure Register API plus the data in the TPP's qualified open-banking certificate before granting account access. [partially-confirmed] | [National Bank of Ukraine - Відкритий банкінг (regulator page); timing statement via Minfin/BorgExpert reporting of NBU Deputy Governor O. Shaban](https://bank.gov.ua/ua/payments/open-banking) | 31/07/2026 | An August-September 2026 system-wide API opening is a hard, dated compliance event for Oschadbank and Raiffeisen Ukraine, not background regulation. Production-grade APIs plus mandatory strong customer authentication, consent capture/revocation, qualified-certificate handling and TPP authorisation checks create attach points for Printec digital onboarding/eKYC, transaction monitoring and fraud control, HSM/key management and AML-compliance tooling. The 'verify the TPP certificate before granting access' obligation in particular is a PKI/HSM workload every ASPSP must stand up. Approach now: banks are inside the readiness window this quarter. | Technical API requirements effective 01/08/2026 (press-sourced, to be confirmed against Resolution No. 80 text); system-wide API opening August-September 2026; full launch autumn 2026; TPP volume ramping thereafter. | Medium | M | Medium | Download the full text of Resolution No. 80 of 25/07/2025 from zakon.rada.gov.ua and pin the EXACT transitional dates for ASPSPs during 2026 - the 01/08/2026 date currently rests on press reporting of an NBU official, not on the resolution text. Enumerate the 6 register entries from the NBU Payment Infrastructure Register (the page's filter list is JS-driven) to confirm whether they are 6 institutions or 3 institutions holding both PISP and AISP roles. Then check whether Oschadbank and Raiffeisen Ukraine have yet published API technical documentation on their own websites - non-publication with a 01/08/2026 deadline is the opening line for a conversation. |

| Raiffeisen banka a.d. Beograd | Serbia | Serbia joined SEPA (Single Euro Payments Area) with effect from 05/05/2026. Annual Report 2025 states: 'Since the Serbian banking system joined SEPA, we have initiated a local SEPA project in collaboration with our headquarters, which has extensive experience implementing SEPA with 12 banks across four countries. Our objective is to become a fully SEPA-compliant bank by May 4, 2026.' IMPORTANT SCOPE CORRECTION: the bank's own customer SEPA Q&A states that in this phase Raiffeisen joins ONLY the SEPA Credit Transfer (SCT) scheme, as do ALL other banks in Serbia, and that all Serbian banks are INDIRECT SEPA participants - RBRS participates through its parent Raiffeisen Bank International AG, Austria, which is the direct participant. SCT is explicitly NOT 24/7: execution is D+1 on business days within defined cycles, with a 13:00 cut-off. SEPA Instant Credit Transfer (SCT Inst, up to 10 seconds) and SEPA Direct Debit (SDD Core/B2B) are described only as the other two SEPA schemes that exist, NOT as schemes Serbia or RBRS is live on. Bosnia and Herzegovina is announced as a new SEPA country from 2027. [partially-confirmed] | [Raiffeisen banka - 'SEPA - pitanja i odgovori o jedinstvenom podrucju placanja u evrima' and Annual Report 2025](https://www.raiffeisenbank.rs/sr/news/2026/vesti/sepa-sistem-pitanja-odgovori.html) | 05/05/2026 | SEPA entry is a real market-wide payments re-platforming event for every Serbian bank (IBAN/ISO 20022 message handling, SHA cost option, routing logic that auto-selects SEPA vs SWIFT, and screening of a growing cross-border EUR flow) and it opens the same conversation at every Serbian bank at once. But the researcher's core commercial premise - a 10-second instant-EUR SLA forcing sub-second sanctions screening and fraud detection - does NOT hold today: Serbia is live on SCT only, which is D+1 with a 13:00 cut-off. The genuine Printec trigger is the FUTURE migration to SCT Inst and SDD, which is not yet dated. Pitch the instant-payments latency argument as a roadmap conversation, not a present requirement, or it will be contradicted by the customer. | SCT in force since 05/05/2026; SCT Inst and SDD adoption in Serbia are undated and are the real future trigger | Medium | M | Medium | Establish the NBS/market timetable for SCT Inst and SDD in Serbia - that date, not 05/05/2026, is the real-time-screening trigger. | UniCredit Bulbank | Bulgaria | CORRECTED - the legal position is solid but must not be tied to the locator's accessibility flags. Directive (EU) 2019/882 (European Accessibility Act), Article 2(1)(b)(ii), expressly lists 'automated teller machines' among self-service terminals in scope, for products placed on the market after 28 June 2025. Article 32(2) provides: 'Member States may provide that self-service terminals lawfully used by service providers for the provision of services before 28 June 2025 may continue to be used in the provision of similar services until the end of their economically useful life, but no longer than 20 years after their entry into use.' Recital wording adds the key condition: they may continue 'as long as they are not replaced during that period'. Separately, and on a DIFFERENT basis, the bank's locator flags 531 of its 761 ATMs as 'Accessible (ground floor/ramp)' and 212 as 'Access with assistance required' - these describe physical access to the site, NOT conformity of the machine's interface. [partially-confirmed] | [Directive (EU) 2019/882 (European Accessibility Act), CELEX 32019L0882, full adopted text; + UniCredit Bulbank locations API](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/eudr/2019/882/adopted/data.html) | 30/07/2026 | Every ATM the bank places on the market from 28 June 2025 onward must meet the Accessibility Act specification, and the transitional relief expires the moment a machine is replaced. That converts the bank's own 'ATM obsolescence' risk flag into a compliance-timed replacement argument rather than a discretionary one, and it means a refresh cannot be done with non-conformant hardware. It does not, however, tell us how many existing Bulbank machines are non-conformant - that is unknown. | Medium - the obligation is certain but bites on replacement, and no Bulgarian enforcement action or ATM accessibility timetable was found | Medium | M | Medium | Check the Bulgarian transposition act and whether any national body has published an ATM accessibility timetable; separately ask the bank how many machines it assesses as EAA-conformant today - the locator cannot answer this. |

| Bank of Albania (regulator) - draft interchange-fee regulation | Albania | The Bank of Albania has a draft regulation in public consultation, 'Projekt-rregullore per tarifat e shkembimit per transaksionet e pagesave te bazuara ne karte' (interchange fees for card-based payment transactions), transposing the EU Interchange Fee Regulation. 'Interchange' is the per-transaction fee the merchant's bank (the acquirer) pays the cardholder's bank (the issuer); it is the largest single component of what a merchant is charged to accept a card. Article 4 of the draft text states verbatim that payment service providers shall not offer or request an interchange fee per transaction higher than (a) 0.2% of transaction value for consumer DEBIT cards and (b) 0.3% for consumer CREDIT cards. CORRECTION: entry into force is NOT left blank as previously recorded - the draft Supervisory Council decision states 'Ky vendim hyn ne fuqi 15 dite pas botimit ne Fletoren Zyrtare' (15 days after publication in the Official Gazette), and separately that the regulation shall remain in force until the date of Albania's accession to the European Union. Adoption is conditional on prior amendment of the Law on Payment Services (law 55/2020), itself still in consultation and not yet submitted to Parliament, which Monitor reports may take several more months. Monitor reports current interchange averages near the 1.5% mark, with the all-in merchant commission reaching up to ~3% on FOREIGN-ISSUED cards; BoA projects the level could fall toward 1%, while banks argue 1.5% is the realistic floor. [partially-confirmed] | [Bank of Albania - draft regulation 'Per tarifat e shkembimit per transaksionet e pagesave te bazuara ne karte' (PDF, 12pp); Revista Monitor - 'Banka e Shqiperise pergatit rregulloren qe do te ule komisionet ndaj tregtareve per pagesat me karte'](https://www.bankofalbania.org/rc/doc/Projektrregullore_p_r_tarifat_e_Shkembimit_34204.pdf) | 22/06/2026 | Interchange caps compress per-transaction acquiring economics, forcing Albanian banks to compete on VOLUME and cost-per-terminal instead of fee margin. That is the classic trigger for outsourcing acceptance: Printec POS/acquiring, Android POS/SoftPOS and POS managed services (estate deployment, TMS, key injection, field service) become cheaper than bank-owned in-house terminal operations. It also raises the payback pressure on every terminal deployed for the 2026 POS mandate. This is a margin/behaviour driver, not a scoped procurement. | High that the caps are adopted as drafted once the Payment Services Law amendment passes; timing is the open variable and the amendment had not reached Parliament as at 22/06/2026 | Medium | Unscoped | Medium | Get the consultation closing date and the Payment Services Law (55/2020) amendment text - the BoA consultation index at /Legjislacioni/Akte_ne_proces_konsultimi/ 302-redirects to an AJAX page and exposes no document links to a plain fetch, so this needs an operator browser session. Then model per-bank acquiring P&L impact and take a POS-as-a-service proposition to Credins, Tirana Bank, Union Bank and BKT as the margin defence. |
| Bank of Albania (regulator) - draft law on payment systems and financial collateral | Albania | A second Bank of Albania draft law is in consultation: 'Projektligj Per sistemin e pagesave dhe kolateralin financiar'. Its explanatory memorandum (relacion) states verbatim that full transposition of amended Directive 98/26/EC and Directive 2002/47/EC forms part of the EU accession Chapter 9 Benchmark. It transposes Directive 98/26/EC on settlement finality in payment and securities settlement systems (as amended by 2009/44/EC, 2010/78/EU and 2019/879/EU) and Directive 2002/47/EC on financial collateral arrangements (as amended by 2009/44/EC and 2014/59/EU), and additionally reflects EMIR (Regulation (EU) 648/2012) and related financial-infrastructure acquis. 'Settlement finality' is the legal rule that once a payment enters a designated system it cannot be unwound, including if a participant fails - it is the precondition for banks to safely settle irrevocably in real time. The draft creates a new, complete and consolidated legal framework for the operation, oversight and legal protection of payment and securities settlement systems, guarantees the security and efficiency of financial collateral and protects collateral-takers' rights if a counterparty enters liquidation, and repeals the existing settlement-finality and financial-collateral provisions. | [Bank of Albania - 'Relacion per projektligjin "Per sistemin e pagesave dhe kolateralin financiar"' (PDF, 10pp)](https://www.bankofalbania.org/rc/doc/Relacion_34400.pdf) | Jul-2026 (retrieved; document itself carries no date on its face) | Settlement-finality law is the legal precondition for the instant-payment scheme and for deeper SEPA integration. Participating banks will need upgraded payment hubs, ISO 20022 messaging and 24x7 liquidity monitoring - Printec transaction processing, HSM/security and managed services, plus AML/transaction monitoring capable of real-time screening (a 24x7 scheme removes the batch window banks currently rely on). This is a legal precondition, not a procurement in itself. | Medium-High: formal consultation stage and tied to an EU accession Chapter 9 benchmark | Medium | Unscoped | Medium | Track adoption in Fletorja Zyrtare and establish the document's actual consultation-publication date (it is undated on its face; Revista Monitor has covered it, which would date the announcement). Map which of the 11 licensed Albanian banks lack a real-time-capable payment hub and approach them before the instant-payments go-live. |

| BCR (Banca Comerciala Romana, Erste Group) | Romania | ACCESSIBILITY GAP CONFIRMED, LEGAL DEADLINE CONTRADICTED. The gap is real and I re-measured it: only 376 of 1,096 BCR ATMs (34%) carry the VOICE (audio guidance) tag, against 572 of 573 MFMs (99.8%); 73 ATMs are not tagged contactless. About 720 BCR ATMs therefore have no audio guidance. Romania's Law 232/2022 (Monitorul Oficial I nr. 743 of 25/07/2022, in force 28/06/2025), which transposes the EU Accessibility Act (Directive 2019/882), expressly covers ATMs ('bancomate' as self-service payment terminals, Art. 2) and requires self-service terminals to provide text-to-speech and support personal headphones (Annex 1). BUT THE RESEARCHER'S 28/06/2030 CLIFF IS WRONG. Art. 29(2) of Law 232/2022 states, verbatim, that self-service terminals used by service providers before 28/06/2025 may continue in use for similar services 'dar nu mai mult de 20 de ani dupa intrarea lor in uz' - no more than 20 years after entry into use. Art. 29(1), which carries the 28/06/2030 date, opens 'Prin exceptie de la prevederile alin. (2)', i.e. it is expressly subordinated to the self-service-terminal rule. Romania took the Member State option in Art. 32(2) of the Directive. So there is NO 2030 replacement cliff for BCR's legacy ATMs; the binding constraint is (a) every terminal placed on the market/put into use from 28/06/2025 must be accessible, and (b) each legacy machine ages out 20 years after ITS OWN entry into use. Peer pressure is nonetheless real: Raiffeisen Bank Romania stated in its Q1-2026 release (04/05/2026) that voice guidance for cash withdrawal at ATMs and MFMs was 'extinsa la nivel national' - extended nationwide. [contradicted] | [Law 232/2022 Art. 29 (Lege5 reproduction of the Monitorul Oficial text) and Directive (EU) 2019/882 Art. 32 (EUR-Lex); BCR locator API](https://lege5.ro/Gratuit/gezdeojwha3tg/legea-nr-232-2022-privind-cerintele-de-accesibilitate-aplicabile-produselor-si-serviciilor) | 31/07/2026 | Roughly 720 BCR ATMs cannot serve blind and partially sighted customers today, against a direct competitor that says it has gone nationwide. That is a reputational and procurement driver, and every unit BCR replaces from now on must ship compliant (text-to-speech, headphone jack) - so accessibility rides on the refresh cycle rather than forcing it. Sellable as an audio/software retrofit where the hardware allows, and as a compliance argument inside any ATM/MFM replacement tender. It is NOT a legally dated 2030 refresh pipeline, and it should not be pitched as one. | Retrofit-or-replace decisions likely tied to normal refresh and to competitive/reputational pressure, not to a statutory 2030 date | Medium | L | High | Establish whether the 720 non-voice ATMs are software/audio-jack upgradeable or need a hardware swap, and get their entry-into-use dates - under Art. 29(2) that date, not 2030, sets each machine's legal end of life. Look for any published BCR accessibility remediation plan (none found this run). |

| National Bank of Serbia / AikBank | Serbia | HARD REGULATORY DEADLINE FOR eKYC, WITH A NEW UNATTENDED-IDENTIFICATION ROUTE. The NBS Executive Board adopted a NEW 'Decision on the conditions and manner of establishing and verifying the identity of a natural person using means of electronic communication' on 07/05/2026 (IO NBS no. 14), published in Official Gazette of the RS no. 43/2026, in force on the eighth day after publication. It REPEALS and replaces the 2019 decision (Gazette 15/2019, 84/2020, 49/2021) - video identification has been legal in Serbia since 2019; what is new is the regime. Two material changes: (1) it creates 'posredna video-identifikacija' - INDIRECT/UNATTENDED video identification, a time-limited real-time recorded session with NO bank employee on the call, permitted only where the technical solution verifies document authenticity and performs a LIVENESS check, and stamps every capture with a QUALIFIED ELECTRONIC TIMESTAMP; (2) it sets prescriptive technical requirements - end-to-end encryption between customer and institution, secure protocols and cryptographic algorithms, high image and sound quality, connection to a reliable time source throughout, and continuous recording of the entire procedure. TRANSITION DEADLINES: institutions already running video identification must align their business and internal acts within SIX MONTHS of entry into force, and adapt their TECHNICAL SOLUTIONS within TWELVE MONTHS. [partially-confirmed] | [National Bank of Serbia - Decision on video identification, Official Gazette RS 43/2026 (full text PDF on nbs.rs), listed on the NBS AML/CFT regulations index](https://www.nbs.rs/export/sites/NBS_site/documents/propisi/propisi-fl/spn_video_identifikacija_p_20260519.pdf) | 07/05/2026 | This is the best-timed compliance play in Serbia. Every Serbian bank already doing video ID must re-engineer its technical solution to a fixed deadline, and the new unattended route is exactly what removes AikBank's staffed 08:00-20:00 constraint. NOTE THE COUNTER-SIGNAL: total operating expenses were -0.5% YoY in H1 but +2.2% YoY in Q2-2026. | [BRD 2Q/H1 2026 Results Presentation, 'COSTS MAINTAINED UNDER STRICT SUPERVISION' slide; BRD Half Year Report June 2026](https://www.brd.ro/sites/default/files/_files/pdf/BRD_FinResPres_Q2%202026.pdf) | 30/07/2026 | Management has publicly committed, in its own words, to substituting technology and a smaller property footprint for headcount, while a doubled turnover tax squeezes the P&L. That is the economic setup a recycler-plus-managed-services proposition monetises: fewer tellers per branch, teller cash handling moved into the machine, less floor space. The Q2 opex uptick (+2.2%) means the savings are being partly reinvested in IT - useful, because it shows the money moves to technology rather than disappearing. | High - stated and quantified management intent | Medium | Unscoped | Medium | Frame the ROI in FTE-equivalents and square metres saved per branch, not in device price - that is the language BRD's own slide uses. Erste Group's H1 2026 interim report, note 31, states that on 06/04/2026 BCR received an investigation report from the Romanian Competition Council alleging agreements and/or concerted practice on coordinated fixing of the ROBOR reference rate among the ten panel banks, covering the period since November 2018; that in early June 2026 the Council publicly announced a fine on BCR of 6.1875% of its 2025 turnover = RON 577,361,887 (~EUR 110m); that the reasoned decision had not been served on BCR; and that BCR will contest it by all available legal means. BCR published its own statements on 08/06/2026 ('Respingem constatarile acesteia si ne vom apara cu fermitate') and 11/06/2026, the latter alleging the Council refused adequate access to the case file and reasonable time to respond. Romanian press reports sector-wide sanctions of RON 3.73bn (~EUR 710m) across ten banks and the National Bank of Romania publicly questioning the decision on financial-stability grounds; those cross-bank figures are press-only and are not in any primary document I could open. | [Erste Group Interim Report H1 2026 note 31; BCR press statements 08/06/2026 and 11/06/2026](https://cdn0.erstegroup.com/content/dam/at/eh/www_erstegroup_com/en/Investor_Relations/2026/reports/IR_Interim_Report_EG_H126.pdf) | 30/07/2026 | A ~EUR 110m provisionable exposure plus a multi-year appeal, on top of an already deteriorating cost/income ratio. ## Verification notes
- **Corrected/contradicted by the adversarial pass (0):** none
- **Unconfirmed (single/weak source, kept at Low) (1):** Alpha Bank
- **Corrected/contradicted by the adversarial pass (4):** UniCredit Bank Romania — CONTRADICTED AND CORRECTED. (1) UniCredit's RON 431.03m is the FOURTH largest fine, not the third - Raiffeisen Bank Romania was fined RON 442.49m, which the original signal omitted entirely. (2) 'BT RON 960m' was wrong: Banca Transilvania's own fine is RON 875.74m; the ~RON 960m figure conflates it with a separate RON 85.03m penalty levied on BT for OTP Bank Romania's conduct, which the Council counts as a distinct entry among the ten. (3) 'Investigation opened end-2022' corrected to October 2022. (4) The UniCredit statement date '17/06/2026' could not be verified; the bank's rejection ('injusta', 'nefundamentata', plus 'Ne exprimam profunda dezamagire fata de caracterul arbitrar al acestui act administrativ') was published in the 08/06/2026 reaction round-up, i.e. within a day of the decision - date corrected. CONFIRMED unchanged: RON 3.73bn / EUR 710m total, ten banks, 07/06/2026, the 60-day action-plan obligation. The Competition Council's own release (consiliulconcurentei.ro) returned 503 to the fetcher and a browser bot-detection interstitial I did not bypass - the per-bank split rests on press, so treat the ranking as press-grade. 'Implies' tightened: the second vendor consequence (surveillance/comms-monitoring budget adjacent to Printec AML) was an unsupported leap and has been removed.; UBB (KBC) / KBC Group — Downloaded and text-extracted the 66-page deck. CONFIRMED from the 'Well-defined core markets' slide: BG = 5% of assets, 2.2m clients, 170 branches, 13bn EUR loans, 16bn EUR deposits. CONFIRMED from the wrap-up slide: '6.1 million users in contact with Kate', '420k converted Kate leads (during last 12 months)', 'KATE autonomy 77% BE 69% CZ'. CONFIRMED: 'Kate has recently been further upgraded in Belgium to enable even more natural and intuitive conversations (Kate 2.0 using LLM)' and 'Kate is built once at a group level and then deployed to all core countries (Kate in a box)'. TWO CORRECTIONS. (1) DATE WRONG: the researcher gave 14/05/2026. KBC released 1Q2026 results on 12/05/2026 (GlobeNewswire, 'KBC Group: First-quarter result of 557 million euros', 12/05/2026, https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2026/05/12/3292472/0/en/KBC-Group-First-quarter-result-of-557-million-euros.html) and the PDF's own metadata gives CreationDate 11/05/2026. source_date corrected to 12/05/2026. (2) SCOPE WRONG: the researcher wrote 'Kate is live in BE, CZ and BG only with roll-out planned to SK and HU'. The 'Four flavours, one Kate' slide actually says 'Kate is available in all KBC's core countries!' - the BE/CZ/BG-only-plus-SK/HU-roll-out statement applies specifically to Kate for SME and corporate clients. Signal text corrected on both points. Verifier also added the end-2025 Bulgarian market-share figures (19% loans and deposits, 15% funds) from the same slide.; Alpha Bank Cyprus Ltd — MATERIAL DATE ERROR. The researcher dated this 22/06/2026 and presented it as 'Reported alongside the VES', implying the 80 exits and 50 Cerberus secondments size the June 2026 scheme. They do not. I opened the Eurofound factsheet directly (as the researcher's own followup suggested) and the raw page is unambiguous: Announcement Date 10 October 2022, employment effect start 10 October 2022, foreseen end date 21 October 2022, sole source 'Stockwatch.com.cy, 12 October 2022', and the Eurofound citation line reads 'Eurofound (2022)'. The factsheet also says the bank 'currently has 17 branches', consistent with 2022 and not with the 23-25 branches it has post-AstroBank. CORRECTED DATE: 10/10/2022. Consequently the researcher's derived statistic - 'an ~80-head reduction on ~746 FTE (roughly 11%)' - is invalid, because it divides a 2022 headcount reduction by a 2026 post-acquisition headcount; I have removed it. This is a live trap: search engines confidently blend this 2022 factsheet into answers about the June 2026 scheme, which is very likely how the error entered. Kept rather than dropped (the underlying facts are real, not fabricated) but relabelled explicitly as precedent, redated, downgraded to Low/S, and the 2016 scheme added from the same factsheet.; UniCredit Bank Romania — CONTRADICTED AND CORRECTED on date and detail. (1) The date '02/03/2026' is wrong: the issue was listed on BVB on 27/03/2026 per the exchange's own press release. (2) 'Outstanding UCB27 and UCB29' is incomplete - UCB28 (2023) is also outstanding, and the new issue is UCB31; four issues are tradeable, totalling RON 2.3bn. Everything the original followup asked for is now answered from the primary exchange source: size RON 600m (upsized from a RON 500m target on oversubscription), tenor to 27/02/2031, coupon 6.82%, EUR 400m ASF-approved MTN programme, proceeds for general financing. SOURCE UPGRADED from the bank's generic press-release listing page to the BVB press release. 'Implies' corrected: the original inference that 'the ROBOR provision is not starving the investment programme' is not supportable, because the bond closed in March 2026 and the fine was issued 07/06/2026.
- **Unconfirmed (single/weak source, kept at Low) (2):** UniCredit Bank Romania, Romanian market (BNR data)

## Access issues & operator requests (per bank)

| bank | Access issue / what failed & the fallback used |
|---|---|
| National Bank of Greece | 1) https://www.nbg.gr/en/group/investor-relations/financial-information/... — 404 via WebFetch; the correct IR path is /en/group/investor-relations/financial-statements-annual-interim/{presentations,results-announcements}. Resolved by scraping the IR index for hrefs with a desktop Chrome User-Agent. Not dropped. 2) https://www.naftemporiki.gr/business/1985632/... — HTTP 403 to WebFetch. Retried the SAME URL with a desktop Chrome User-Agent via curl and got HTTP 200 plus the full article body and a machine-readable datePublished of 2025-07-24. The UA retry route worked exactly as the access guidance predicts. Not dropped, and used as a cited source. 3) https://www.nbg.gr/en/group/human-resources/career-opportunities/jobs-nbg — JS-paginated. The listing reports "1 / 6" pages but ?page=N, ?p=N, ?pageNo=N and /N all return the same first five records (Job IDs 3204-3209, all Finance/Treasury/Operations/Retail roles, none IT or self-service). Only 5 of ~30 postings were readable. I did NOT escalate to Claude-in-Chrome because the visible slice contained no technology roles and the expected yield was low relative to cost — logging it as a deliberate, bounded gap rather than a silent drop. Not an access failure, but no signal extracted. 7) pdftotext was not present on the machine and pip3 initially refused to install pdfminer.six under PEP 668; installed with --break-system-packages into the scratchpad workflow. All PDFs in this pass (Pillar 3 Q1 2026, 1Q26 presentation, Qivalis release, AFR 2025) were extracted locally rather than through the fetch tool, per the access guidance. |

| Raiffeisen banka Serbia | www.printecgroup.com/raiffeisen-serbia-branch-transformation/ returns HTTP 404 even with a desktop User-Agent - logged, and the surviving Printec asset at offer.printecgroup.com was used instead (that one 403'd on the first WebFetch and served on retry). nbs.rs statistics pages (/sr/drugi-nivo-navigacije/statistika/platni_sistem/ and /sr/finansijske-institucije/banke/platne-kartice/) are JS-rendered and return only CSS/font links to curl with a desktop UA, so NBS figures are cited via Tanjug and via the nbs.rs showcontent article URLs that do render - logged rather than dropped. www.raiffeisenbank.rs/sr/o-nama.html and /sr/o-nama/investitori/finansijski-izvestaji.html are 404; the live reports index is /sr/common/izvestaji.html. The AR 2025 PDF could not be text-extracted by pdfminer's one-shot high_level call within the tool timeout and had to be run as a background job. Note: the shared scratchpad is used concurrently by other agents and a generically named ar2025.pdf was overwritten mid-run, so all Raiffeisen Serbia artefacts were re-downloaded into a dedicated subdirectory. No source was silently dropped. |
| AikBank / AikGroup | 1) printecgroup.com returned HTTP 403 to the fetch tool; resolved by retrying the same URL with a desktop-browser User-Agent via curl. 2) aikbank.rs serves its PDFs gzip-encoded with a .pdf extension, so pdfminer failed with 'No /Root object' until the file was gunzipped — worth remembering for future AikBank runs. 3) zse.hr/en/issuer/166 returns 404; the working primary path for Podravska banka is https://zse.hr/hr/papir/310?isin=HRPDBARA0008. 4) www.nbs.rs served fine once a desktop User-Agent was used; its legacy .xls statistics files (broj_i_promet_bankomati.xls etc.) are BIFF format and unreadable with openpyxl, but the parallel .xlsx files under /statistika/el_novac/ carry the same series and were used instead. 5) NOT SOLVED — AikBank's branch/ATM locator at https://www.aikbank.rs/lokacije/ is an Adobe AEM edge-delivery JavaScript app; /lokacije.json, /lokacije/lokacije.json, /lokacije/locations.json and /query-index.json all return 404 and scripts.js exposes no data endpoint, so the ATM estate could not be enumerated machine-readably. Escalation to a browser session was not attempted this run and is the logical next step. 6) The AikBank news article pages render their body server-side but only on the exact trailing-slash URL; a first pass with a different UA string returned empty bodies and was retried successfully. 7) The NBS quarterly payment press release reachable via search (id=18334) turned out to be a 2022 item, so the current figures were taken from the statistics spreadsheets rather than the news page. |
| Hungary (OTP, K&H, Erste HU, MBH, Raiffeisen HU, CIB, Granit) | kh.hu returns HTTP 403 (Akamai edge block, reference #18.66451502.x) to both the fetch tool and to desktop-UA curl with Hungarian Accept-Language. Route taken: desktop-UA curl retry (failed), then press mirrors (piacesprofit.hu, mfor.hu) — which proved to be 2019 material and were therefore discarded, so K&H's current deposit-ATM and automated-branch figures are unconfirmed and deliberately NOT reported. njt.hu/jogszabaly/2025-19-20-2C returned ECONNRESET; routed around it via the net.jogtar.hu consolidated text of the same decree. Route taken: desktop-UA curl retry (failed), then press mirrors (piacesprofit.hu, mfor.hu) — which proved to be 2019 material and were therefore discarded, so K&H's current deposit-ATM and automated-branch figures are unconfirmed and deliberately NOT reported. njt.hu/jogszabaly/2025-19-20-2C returned ECONNRESET; routed around it via the net.jogtar.hu consolidated text of the same decree. All mnb.hu decision PDFs return raw binary to the fetch tool. Route used: desktop-UA curl to disk, then local text extraction with pdfminer.six (pdftotext is not installed in this environment). The same route was used for the Raiffeisen-hosted decision PDFs. MNB's kozerdeku-adatok decision register only publishes H-KPL and H-STA series; AML (H-PM) and consumer-protection (H-FK) decisions are not listed there and had to be reached through the banks' own statutory publication pages. |
| Slovenia (NLB, OTP banka d.d., Intesa SI, Bankart, Addiko SI) | ibm.com/case-studies/bankart returned HTTP 403 to the fetch tool; recovered by re-requesting the same URL with a desktop Chrome User-Agent via curl and stripping the HTML locally (HTTP 200). enarocanje.si/Obrazci query URL returned 404 and two guessed bsi.si statistics paths returned 404; routed instead to the OPSI open-data catalogue entry and then to the raw PX-Axis file at px.bsi.si, which succeeded. pdftotext is not installed in this environment - all PDFs were extracted with Python pdfminer instead. Slovenian public procurement portals carry no relevant bank ATM/POS notices, which is expected: NLB, OTP SI, Intesa SI and Bankart are not contracting authorities for this spend, so no tender records exist to mine. No source was silently dropped. |
| Albania (Raiffeisen AL, BKT, Credins, OTP AL, Tirana Bank, Union Bank) | bankofalbania.org returns HTTP 403 to WebFetch on every path. Route used: curl with a desktop Chrome User-Agent, which worked on all BoA pages, plus local PDF text extraction (pdftotext) for the draft regulation and explanatory memorandum. Logged blocked URLs and routes: https://www.bankofalbania.org/Payments/Payment_systems_statistics/ — 403 to WebFetch, retrieved via UA curl. https://www.unionbank.al/programi-posi-nga-mastercard/ — Cloudflare "Attention Required" block on UA curl AND 403 on WebFetch; NOT retrieved. Worked around by using Raiffeisen Bank Albania's equivalent POSÍ page, so the programme terms cited are Raiffeisen's, not Union Bank's. https://blog.printecgroup.com/printec-albania-enters-a-new-era-with-its-new-offices-in-tirana — 403 to WebFetch and empty body to UA curl; NOT retrieved, so that item is cited at Low confidence off the search snippet only. https://otpbank.al/en/branches-and-atms/ — fetched (420KB) but the branch/ATM locator is JavaScript-rendered, so units could not be counted; the About page was used instead. https://openprocurement.al/sq/albaniandf/list?search=... — returns HTTP 200 but ignores the search parameter and serves the default listing, so no keyword procurement search was possible. BoA publication indexes (/Botime/Botime_Periodike/Raporti_Vjetor_i_Mbikeqyrjes/) are AJAX-paginated and expose no PDF hrefs to a plain fetch. monitor.al articles are partially paywalled; only the visible portion was used. Nothing was silently dropped. |

**Operator requests — gated / paywalled / blocked sources to fetch or verify manually:**
- **National Bank of Greece:** No hard paywall or login gate was hit this run, so there is nothing an operator must buy or authenticate to reach. Two optional operator-assisted items, both browser-rather-than-paywall problems: 1) NBG careers listing — https://www.nbg.gr/en/group/human-resources/career-opportunities/jobs-nbg. JS pagination hides roughly 25 of ~30 open postings from plain HTTP scraping (see access_issues item 3). A short browser session paging through all six pages would confirm or rule out hiring for Finacle/core-banking run-phase, ATM/self-service channel, AML and HSM/security roles — the cheapest available proxy for which internal programmes are actually being staffed after the May-2026 go-live. Low urgency. 2) NBG AGM of 30/04/2026 — the technology remarks by Chairman Hardouvelis and CEO Mylonas (five-year CBS project, in-branch customer-management system upgrade, loan origination, cloud, cybersecurity, 70+ AI staff) currently rest on Business Daily's report. If NBG publishes AGM minutes or a webcast recording, an operator retrieving the primary record would let the Orchestrator lift that row from press to primary tier and pin exact programme scope. Also note for the next run rather than for an operator: NBG's 1H26 results, presentation and interim financial statements were due on the IR site after the Euronext Athens close on 30/07/2026 and should simply be re-pulled on 31/07/2026 — no operator involvement needed, just timing.
- **Piraeus Financial Holdings:** 1) Piraeus H1 2026 conference call audio — https://www.piraeusgroup.gr/-/jssmedia/Com/2026/Files/Investors/Financials/Financial-Results/H1/piraeus260729.mp3 (29/07/2026): please have this transcribed, or obtain the paywalled Refinitiv/Bloomberg transcript. Target the analyst Q&A on branch network strategy, ATM estate plans, the composition of the ~EUR140mn IT capex, and any commentary on Cashflex economics. 2) Piraeus Six-Month 2026 Consolidated Financial Report — flagged in the press release as "expected to be available on the Bank's website" but not yet posted at time of research; please retrieve once published (notes on property/equipment additions and intangibles would help size the channel-hardware share). 3) H1 2026 Financial Factsheet XLSX — https://www.piraeusgroup.gr/-/jssmedia/Com/2026/Files/Investors/Financials/Financial-Results/H1/IRTool-Website_H1_2026.xlsx: worth downloading for the full historical KPI series (branches, e-banking clients, Snappi users) rather than the four quarters printed in the press release.
- **Eurobank Holdings (+ Postbank BG, Eurobank Cyprus):** 1) Eurobank Holdings FY2025 Annual Financial Report PDF (c.7.0 MB, the full statutory report, not the results deck) - download and text-extract offline; it is the only document likely to carry a per-country ATM count and a branch-property/leasehold note. Flagged in the previous run too; extraction exceeded the run's time budget again. 2) 'The Cyprus Journal of Wealth Management' 2026 edition, Gold magazine / magloft reader (https://www.magloft.com/app/goldmagazine#/reader/532014/1993828) - contains the full Haris Hambakis (Eurobank Limited) interview; the CBN web version is truncated and image-encoded. Behind a Gold/IMH subscription (EUR 40/yr). 3) Central Bank of Cyprus 'Payment Statistics: second half of 2025' - the published PDF at centralbank.cy is corrupt (41 KB, no PDF root). Request a clean copy from CBC statistics, or the underlying tables, to get the current Cyprus ATM/POS counts. 4) Bulgarian National Bank / BORICA per-institution ATM and POS terminal counts for 2026 - not published openly; needed to size Postbank's share of the Bulgarian estate post-euro. 5) The Eurobank 'Banking Forward' press release of 29/06/2026 - not reachable on eurobank.gr (no pagination on the press-office index, every dated slug 404s). Ask Eurobank IR/press for the release PDF, or check the printed/emailed distribution.
- **Alpha Bank (Greece):** 1) Bank of Greece, 'Etisia Ekthesi Drastiriotiton Proliptikis Epopteias kai Exygiansis 2025' (Annual Report on Preventive Supervision and Resolution Activities 2025), published 28/07/2026 — the PDF sits behind a JS-rendered listing on bankofgreece.gr. An operator opening the page in a real browser should grab the PDF and confirm (a) whether the credit institution fined EUR 1,891,050 for 13 AML/CFT breaches is named or identifiable, and (b) the exact wording of the transaction-monitoring findings. This would upgrade the AML row from press to regulator tier and materially sharpen the Siron pitch. 2) Printec's own blog post 'Printec and Alpha Bank implement Instant Payments ensuring money transfer and availability in 0.7 seconds' is Cloudflare-403 from this environment; an operator on a normal browser should capture the publication date and scope so the Printec-Alpha incumbency evidence can be cited properly. 3) Alpha Bank H1 2026 results pack, published before market open on 31/07/2026 (press release, presentation and interim financial statements at alpha.gr/en/group/investor-relations/group-results-and-reporting) — worth an immediate operator pull tomorrow morning for IT/G&A expense lines, branch count, headcount, any self-service or digital KPIs, the H2 2026 Investor Day date, and any primary confirmation of the reported >EUR 100mn annual technology investment.
- **Optima Bank:** No paywalled or gated PDFs were encountered — the entire Optima primary pack is open-access and was downloaded successfully. Two JS-rendered pages would benefit from a Claude-in-Chrome pass by an operator (both are free, no login, just Angular-rendered): (1) https://www.optimabank.gr/en/about-us/newsroom/ — enumerate 2026 press releases, specifically anything on technology partnerships, card/payment product launches, branch openings or ATM changes; (2) https://www.optimabank.gr/en/about-us/human-resources/theseis-ergasias/ — capture the full current job list, filtering the Technology and Compliance departments, since open DLP/network-security/AML requisitions would corroborate the two disclosed open vendor selections and give a named hiring manager. Optionally (3) the Bank of Greece payment statistics XLSX on data.gov.gr (https://data.gov.gr/organization/trapeza-tis-ellados?res_format=XLSX) for the Greek national ATM/terminal installed base, which would let a future run size Optima's ~30 machines against the market.
- **CrediaBank (ex-Attica):** None strictly required — every source cited was opened successfully. Two optional operator pickups: (a) on/after 04-05/08/2026, fetch the next Malta Stock Exchange announcement in the series (likely https://cdn.borzamalta.com.mt/download/announcements/HSBC480.pdf or HSBC481.pdf) for HSBC Bank Malta's H1-2026 interim accounts and any further separation/IT write-down disclosure; (b) on 06/08/2026, fetch the CrediaBank H1-2026 results presentation PDF from https://www.crediabank.com/omilos/enimerosi-ependuton/etairikes-parousiaseis/ (binary — needs local pdfminer extraction) for the updated branch-refurbishment count, ATM count and Euronet/Malta closing status.
- **Bank of Cyprus:** 1) Association of Cyprus Banks Annual Report 2025-2026 PDF - the source of the BOC 56 branches / 132 ATMs / 2,447 staff figures and the 155 / 321 / 6,253 sector totals. It is not linked on acb.com.cy/publications/annual-reports (latest listed there is 2023-2024) and was only available second-hand via CBN. 2) The Printec case study "Transforming through Teller Cash Recyclers: Bank of Cyprus" - referenced in search indexes of printecgroup.com/resources, but that listing renders client-side and the document itself was not reachable; it is the most likely place the TCR OEM is named, which would settle the standing Glory-incumbency question. 3) The full text of the Cyprus Tenders Review Authority decision of 15/07/2026 in JCC Payment Systems v VivaBank on the government e-payments tender, for the contract value and the transition timetable.
- **Eurobank Limited (ex-Hellenic) Cyprus:** 1) Eurobank Limited (Cyprus) Pillar III Disclosures for the year ended 31/12/2025 — https://www.eurobank.cy/-/media/hbc/pillar-3-disclosures/eurobank-limited/pillar-iii-disclosures-for-the-year-ended-31-december-2025.pdf — Cloudflare 403 to every automated route including Claude-in-Chrome text extraction; needs a human browser download. 2) Eurobank Limited (Cyprus) standalone statutory Report and Financial Statements FY2025 (and 1H2026 if published) — wanted for the Cyprus IT/intangibles capex line, software additions and any named integration provision, which would size the ATM/POS/core-banking spend. 3) Association of Cyprus Banks Annual Report 2025-2026 PDF — to confirm the 59 branches / 156 ATMs / 2,450 staff figures for the merged Eurobank at the start of 2026 and to obtain any per-bank self-service breakdown. 4) STANDING VENDOR TASK: identify the OEM behind Hellenic/Eurobank 'Cash 360' note and coin machines and the current installed count. Best routes are a branch site visit (machine fascia branding at the Nicosia Makarios III Ave, Limassol Agia Fylaxis/Gladstonos, Larnaca Zenon Kitionos or Paphos Geroskipou automated service points), a Cyprus company-registry supplier filing, or a direct approach to Eurobank Limited procurement.
